# TikTok engagement log (@ngeske)

Tech-focused engagement passes for PING Social brand building (Nolan's 2026-09-15 directive: TikTok too, same playbook as the other platforms; refined same day: tech-related content ONLY).

## Header rules (read before every pass)

- Targets: tech-related content ONLY (tech leaders, founders, builders, tech/startup content). The earlier trades-first plan is dropped per Nolan's 2026-09-15 refinement. Creator and business accounts only; never personal profiles that are not business-related.
- Creator priority: work the seed list top-down, most popular first: ~/workspace/outreach/tech-creator-seed-list.md (v1, rebuilt 2026-09-15 from Nolan's Spotify listening history: 189 followed shows + 71 saved episodes mapped; Spotify signals outrank X/Threads). Engage ONLY videos posted by VERIFIED handles themselves. Never engage impersonator, squatter, or fan-clip accounts for creators with no personal TikTok (notably the fake Elon Musk accounts and the @stevenbartlett squatter). Web-search discovery remains the backfill for fresh tech content beyond the list.
- Likes AND comments. Like tech videos that resonate; comment smart, specific, funny, under ~20 words, reacting to something specific in the video. Natural @mentions where fitting.
- NEVER sell. No PING Social mention, no links, no contact info, no pitching. The comments exist to spark replies and profile views, never to pitch.
- NEVER re-engage a handled handle or video. The guard script enforces this mechanically: `~/workspace/outreach/engagement-guard.py` (platform `tiktok`) consults this file's durable history. Prose is not the enforcement. Video identity is the numeric id from tiktok.com/@<handle>/video/<id> URLs.
- Caps per pass (Nolan's 2026-09-15 double-down order: 60 likes + 20 comments to start, ramping quick like Threads): up to 60 likes + 20 comments, every 4h. Halved to 30 likes + 10 comments 2026-09-15 19:35 MDT after a driver-reported possible-checkpoint abort; RESTORED to 60+20 by the 2026-09-15 night watch after the root cause was proven to be a TikTok layout change (selectors fixed in tk-engage.js, verified by real like/unlike state change) and the "checkpoint" abort was proven a BAD-regex false positive on the word "slow down" in the video's own caption. RE-HALVED to 30+10 by the 2026-09-16 00:50 night watch after Pass 3 (23:45-00:35 MDT): first 5 likes applied cleanly, then 9 consecutive likes clicked but never applied while all 11 comments executed cleanly, a soft like-rate-limit signal on the session. HALVED AGAIN to 15 likes + 5 comments by the 2026-09-16 03:55 night watch after Pass 4: driver executed one like cleanly then self-aborted with "possible checkpoint, rate limit, or block detected". Two consecutive soft rate-limit signals. Full restore needs Nolan's morning review. HALVED AGAIN to 7 likes + 2 comments by the 2026-09-16 08:07 worker after Pass 5: third consecutive soft like-rate-limit signal (1 like applied, 6 consecutive like failures with "click sent, no aria-pressed=true observed", while all 3 comments executed cleanly). Comments are the reliable action on TikTok right now; likes are throttled to ~1-5 per pass. Full restore still needs Nolan's morning review. Pacing: 60 to 120 seconds between actions. Never exceed caps. TikTok's bot detection is aggressive; keep interactions minimal, human-paced, and never burst.
- Execution runs on the Pig's headed TikTok Chrome (tt-profile, CDP :9227) via ~/workspace/pig-fb/tk-engage.js, driven by ~/workspace/pig-fb/run_tk_pass.sh. ONE driver at a time on the TikTok Chrome. Parallelize across platforms, never within a pass.
- Stop instantly on any action block, checkpoint, verification challenge, rate-limit warning, or sign of reduced reach. Halve next pass's caps and surface it to Nolan immediately.
- Stay-on rule: this log's passes stay scheduled unless there is a real blocker or genuine flagged-account risk. A down CDP or logged-out session is NOT a reason to stop the schedule: end that pass with zero engagement, log it, keep going next pass. Disable only on an actual restriction/action block/checkpoint, or Nolan saying stop.
- Quiet runs: only surface to Nolan on block, checkpoint, login failure, guard fail-closed, or a verify VIOLATION. Otherwise report only pass counts.

## Like-throttle machinery rules (2026-09-24 P2 lane; read before every pass)

- Like evidence: tk-engage.js now records per-like evidence (element identity, aria-pressed before/after with polls at +2s/+5s/+10s, remount detection via a DOM marker, like-count change, restriction-surface scan) and a deterministic fail_class: LIKE_APPLIED / LIKE_APPLIED_LATE / ALREADY_LIKED / SELECTOR_NOT_FOUND_ABSENT / SELECTOR_NOT_FOUND_HIDDEN / NO_STATE_CHANGE_UNKNOWN / NO_STATE_CHANGE_REMOUNT / NO_STATE_CHANGE_RESTRICTED. A failed like must carry one of these signatures; "click sent, no state change" alone is no longer an acceptable note. Cause stays UNKNOWN unless the evidence discriminates: do not call a like failure throttling without a restriction-surface match or equivalent platform evidence.
- Restriction detection: the driver scans ONLY page chrome (body children EXCLUDING the video article subtree, where captions live). A caption containing "slow down" cannot trip it (2026-09-15 false positive, now a node regression test: ~/workspace/outreach/tests/test_tk_restriction_scan.js). Every abort record carries the matched phrase and a snippet; a detected restriction becomes BLOCKED, never an optimization problem.
- Durable like state: ~/workspace/outreach/tiktok-like-recovery-state.json is the like SAFETY tripwire (CLEAR = likes allowed at ramp volumes, BLOCKED = likes frozen). 2026-09-28 CORRECTION (Nolan, supersedes the 2026-09-24 probe machinery and the 06:30 freeze): the automatic like-restoration probe is PERMANENTLY DEAD - no 1-like probes, no probe cadence, no clean-probe counting, no cap ladder, no automatic restoration. Before building a pass, run `python3 ~/workspace/outreach/tiktok-like-recovery.py plan-next` and obey likes_allowed (false means 0 likes this pass). After each pass, run `record-pass --pass-id <id> --driver-result <file>`; an explicit restriction signal re-BLOCKS, and only a recorded review (`record-review --decision allow`) clears it. Like VOLUMES come from the ramp policy below, never from this script.
- Ramp policy (Nolan's 2026-09-28 corrected directive): pass 89 stays as configured/scaled back. Starting with the next pass, resume ALL interactions at flag-safe scaled-back volumes: 5 likes + 2 comments per pass. Weekly ramp: +5 likes and +3 comments per week, up to the standing ceiling. All safety tripwires stay active: any warning, checkpoint, or restriction drops immediately to minimum (0 likes + 0 comments) and re-BLOCKS likes via the tripwire above.
- Classification: run ~/workspace/outreach/tiktok-failure-classify.py on the driver result. Log the classifier's like_verdict and comment_verdict separately; overall pass classification must not mask like-specific failures (a pass where comments landed but likes failed is PARTIAL_LIKE_FAILURE, never SUCCESS_ACTION). Worker pass Classification lines must use the classifier's suggested_pass_class.

## Night-watch correction 2026-09-15 ~21:00 MDT (guard identity semantics)

- Correction to the Pass 2 note above ("keys on account+action, NOT identity+action"): for tiktok/instagram/threads/x the account handle IS the identity (durable never-re-engage is handle-level: "handle @x engaged in a prior pass", plus post-URL shortcodes). The guard's in-run (identity, action) dedup therefore behaves as (handle, action), which is the documented behavior, not a deviation. The code comment in engagement-guard.py now states this explicitly. Verified by a deterministic test (~/workspace/outreach/tests/test_guard_inrun_dedup.py, 4/4 green): second like on a different video of the same handle is blocked in-run; like+comment pairs pass. Planning guidance stands: spread candidates across many handles, not many videos per handle.
- TikTok selector fix (same watch): tk-engage.js like/comment selectors updated for TikTok's layout change (button[aria-label=...] -> div[role=button][data-e2e=like-icon]/[data-e2e=comment-icon], aria-pressed on the div). Verified by real state change on https://www.tiktok.com/@simonshipsai/video/7614919565482544405: pressed false->true (like) ->false (unlike); comment icon/input/submit selectors all live-verified. Deployed to the Pig. No soft like-block (actions register cleanly). 19:35 "possible checkpoint" abort reproduced as a BAD-regex false positive: the word "slow down" appears in that video's own caption ("you need to slow down and guide it").
